   * @return {boolean} - if the event was a valid isolation message
   * @type {string}
   * @type {{queue: object[], ready: boolean, frame: HTMLElement | null}}
   * A runtime generated key to ensure an IPC call comes from an initialized frame.

   * IMPORTANT: See isolation_secure.js for the isolation frame implementation.

?vision.check_mapVisually check a rendered mapVisual QA of a rendered map / chart image before delivering it. It OCRs the image and compares against expected_texts (every title, legend entry, label, scale and source text you drew) to find text that is missing, rendered as boxes (missing CJK glyphs), garbled, truncated or covered; flags overlapping labels; and, when a multimodal model is available, asks it to review layout, overlaps, missing map elements and readability. Read a fileRead a text file (UTF-8, with a GBK fallback for legacy Chinese files). `path` must be absolute. Returns a numbered line range using 1-based `offset` and `limit` (max 500). It stops after the requested page by default, so large files are not scanned in full; when `truncated=true`, request the next offset. Set `include_hash=true` only immediately before editing when an `expected_hash` is needed; this intentionally scans the full file and also returns an exact total line count. Binary files are rejected. PDF / Word (.pdf/.docx) documents: use doc.read. Spatial files (.tif/.tiff/.shp/.gpkg/.img/.dem/.nc etc.) are NOT supported

When the request is about the document's content, read it with doc.read path=<that path> (if it is outside the accessible directories, call fs.authorize_path with permission=read first); for long documents read the pages you need and continue with next_offset. Scanned pages are recognized by local OCR when it is enabled (status ocr; may contain recognition errors); pages reported as no_text could not be read: tell the user which pages were skipped. Never invent the document's content, and do not use fs.read, sandbox.exec or bash to parse these files.

Tell the user plainly that it is not a supported image file (supported: PNG / JPG / WebP / BMP / TIF) and ask them to provide one in a supported format. doc.readRead a PDF or Word documentRead the text of a PDF (.pdf) or Word (.docx) document. `path` must be absolute (files the user drops into the chat arrive as paths in the message; a path outside the accessible directories must be authorized with fs.authorize_path, permission=read, first). PDFs are returned page by page; .docx is returned in parts of about 8000 characters. `offset` is the first page/part (1-based, default 1) and `limit` how many to return (default 10, max 50); output also stops early at about 40000 characters, and after a few scanned pages (OCR takes seconds per page). When `next_offset` is present, more content remains

fs.authorize_pat
fs.authorize_pathAuthorize a file pathAsk the user to authorize access to an absolute file or directory path outside the current conversation assets and current project source folders. If the path is a file or a new file path, its existing parent directory is authorized. In a project conversation it is saved as a read-only project source folder; generated files must still be written to the current conversation asset folders. Outside projects it is saved for the current conversation with the requested permission. Call this before fs.read/fs.list/fs.glob/fs.grep/sandbox.exec when the user provided a path outside the accessible directories. Only the user can widen what you may access: a request is accepted only for a path the user dragged in or wrote in this conversation; any other path is refused without asking the user. The user confirms each new location by hand, also in auto-approve mode; a location already granted is not asked again. Request only the specific folder the task needs, never a drive root, the user home, Desktop or Documents as a whole, and never a folder just to look around. When you do not know where a file is, ask the user to drag it in or give its path instead of searching the disk. If the request is refused or rejected, tell the user plainly and continue with what is accessible.absolute file or directory path requested by the useroptional short display nameread_write@

job.cancelCancel a background jobCancel a running background job of this conversation (kills the tool process). Use it when the user asks to stop it or the job is no longer needed. Work the job already did (files written, rows changed) is NOT rolled back 
job.listList background jobs of this conversationList the background jobs of this conversation: long-running tool calls that returned `status: running` with a job_id and kept running in the background. Returns id, tool, status (running / succeeded / failed / cancelled / interrupted), progress and elapsed time. Finished jobs are included so their results can be looked up with job_status. Use it when you lost track of a job_id.crates\opengeodex-app\src\ops\background_jobs.rs
job.statusGet one background jobGet the status of one background job by job_id. While running: progress and elapsed time. When finished: the full result (succeeded) or the error (failed / cancelled / interrupted). Do NOT poll this in a loop 
job.waitWait for a background jobBlock until a background job finishes or max_seconds elapses (at most 60; default 30), then return its status. Use it ONLY when the next step truly needs the result and the job should finish soon. If the job is still running afterwards, tell the user it continues in the background and end your turn

sandbox.execRun a sandboxed scriptRun a python/node/gitbash/shell(cmd)/powershell snippet in a restricted sandbox. Python/Node.js/Git Bash prefer OpenGeodex bundled runtimes when they exist; install missing Python packages with python.env_install before calling this tool. Do not use `packages`: sandbox.exec never installs dependencies itself. Runtime choice: use python for structured data or geospatial scripting, node for JavaScript tooling, gitbash for POSIX shell workflows such as sed/awk/find/tar/gzip/curl and portable batch file operations, shell(cmd) only for Windows cmd built-ins, and powershell only for PowerShell-specific Windows automation. The bundled Git Bash includes curl; use `runtime:"gitbash"` for HTTP HEAD checks, Range requests, resumable downloads, and simple API debugging when no dedicated OpenGeodex download/fetch tool fits. **File access discipline (important):** scripts may read absolute file paths inside the current conversation assets, the current project's source folders, or paths already authorized by the user. Project folders are read-only references. If the user gives an external path, call fs.authorize_path first, then run sandbox.exec. **Write discipline (important):** on Windows the sandbox runs under a restricted + low-integrity token, so the script can ONLY write its own temp working dir (the cwd). Writing to absolute paths from inside the script

skill.loadLoad a skillLoad a skill's full instructions by name 
skill.suggestSuggest a reusable skillPropose a reusable user skill WITHOUT saving it. Call this near the end of a successful task only when you, the model, found a repeatable workflow worth preserving, especially after detours, failed attempts, tricky parameters, or domain-specific validation. The UI shows a 'save as skill' card and the user decides whether to save; never claim the skill is saved. When the user explicitly asks to save the procedure as a skill, use skill.create instead. Do not call for ordinary one-off chat, trivial tasks, or a procedure that merely records this turn's exact files. Reuse the existing skill name when improving a known skill instead of creating near-duplicates. name = short recognizable title; trigger_condition = future task pattern, not the current user's exact wording; body = concise Markdown instructions for future reuse, including tool order, key parameters, validation checks, and common failure fixes. Never include current-session data, absolute file paths, concrete output filenames, or one-off region/project facts; use placeholders like <input_path>, <area>, <output_dir>. reason = why this should be saved.short skill namewhen this skill should be used in futurereusable Markdown instructionsshort reason shown to the user
